22: 
23:                                                                        1. Introduction
24:                                          Given a directed graph G = (V, E) an independent set A ⊂ V is called
25:                                          quasi-kernel (quasi-sink) iff for each point v there is a path of length
26:                                          at most 2 from some point of A to v (from v to some point of A). (The
27:                                          notions have a fairly extensive literature: as a starting point see for
28:                                          example the following papers: [2], [3], [4].)
29:                                            The starting point of our investigation was the following theorem:
30:                                          Theorem 1.1 (Chvátal- Lovász, [1]). Every finite digraph (i.e. directed
31:                                          graph) contains a quasi-kernel.
...
32:                                          Our aim is to find similar theorems for infinite digraphs. The plain
33:                                          generalization of Theorem 1.1 fails even for infinite tournaments: the
34:                                          tournament (Z, <) is a counterexample, where Z denotes the set of the
35:                                          integers, and (x, y) is an edge iff x < y. However, not just for (Z, <)
36:                                          but for each tournament G we have a partition (V0 , V1 ) of the vertex set
37:                                          of the tournament such that the induced subgraph G[V0 ] has a quasi-
38:                                          kernel and the induced subgraph G[V1 ] has a quasi sink, see Theorem
39:                                          3.1. Moreover, all the infinite digraph we could construct have this
40:                                          property. These observations led to formulate the following conjecture.
41:                                            2000 Mathematics Subject Classification. 05C20,05C69.
42:                                            Key words and phrases. infinite directed graph, quasi-kernel, quasi-sink.
43:                                            The preparation of this paper was partly supported by the Hungarian NSF,
44:                                          under contract Nos. T 37758, T37846, T61600, NK62321, AT048826.
45:                                                                                   1
46: 
47: 2                      P. L. ERDŐS AND L. SOUKUP
48: 
49: Conjecture 1.2. Given any digraph G = (V, E) one can find a parti-
50: tion (V0 , V1 ) of the vertex set such that the induced subgraph G[V0 ] has
51: a quasi-kernel and G[V1 ] has a quasi-sink.
52:    The paper is organized as follows. In Section 2 we prove some easy
...
52:    The paper is organized as follows. In Section 2 we prove some easy
53: results showing that digraphs that resemble to a finite graph have quasi-
54: kernels: digraphs with finite in-degrees (Corollary 2.1) and digraphs
55: with finite chromatic number (Corollary 2.4) have quasi-kernels.
56:    We prove the conjecture above for digraphs that resemble to a tour-
57: nament (Theorems 3.2 and 3.5), or that are built up from simple blocks
58: (Corollary 2.6).
59:    In Section 4 we study the structures of infinite tournaments, espe-
60: cially of tournaments without quasi-kernels. For n ∈ N denote Outn the
61: family of digraphs G = (V, E) having an independent set A ⊂ V such
62: that for each point v there is a path of length at most n from some point
63: of A to v. In Section 4 for each n ≥ 3 we could characterize infinite
64: tournaments from Outn , see Theorem 4.2. This characterization will
65: imply immediately that the classes Out3 , Out4 , . . . contain the same
66: tournaments! On the other hand, we show that Out2 and Out3 con-
67: tain different tournaments (see Theorem 4.3), but the proof demands
68: the development a recursive method to construct infinite digraphs from
69: certain finites ones (see Section 5). One could hope that this method
70: may help to disprove our conjecture, but this is not the case, because,
71: in Theorem 5.5 we will show that all the digraphs obtained by this
72: method also satisfy the Conjecture 1.2 above.
...
115: V = InG  n (A), and let G ∈ Outn iff there is an independent set B ⊂ V
116: such that V = OutG    n (B). We will say that “A witnesses G ∈ Inn ” and
117: “B witnesses G ∈ Outn ”.
118:    If n, k ∈ N∪{∞} define the class Inn -Outk of digraphs as follows: let
119: G ∈ Inn -Outk if and only if there is a partition (V1 , V2 ) of the vertex set
120: V such that G[V1 ] ∈ Inn and G[V2 ] ∈ Outk . We will say that “(V1 , V2 )
121: witnesses G ∈ Inn -Outk ”.
122: Using this new terminology we can formulate Theorem of Chvátal and
123: Lovász and our Conjecture as follows:
124: Theorem 1.1 . Every finite digraph is in Out2 ,
125: Conjecture 1.2 . Every digraph is in In2 -Out2 .

128: get the following straightforward corollary of Theorem 1.1 for infinite
129: graphs:
130: Corollary 2.1. If every vertex has finite in-degree in a digraph G then
131: G has a quasi-kernel.
132:   Next we prove two stepping-up theorems. The first will imply im-
133: mediately that every finitely chromatic digraph has quasi-kernel. The
134: second one will be applied mainly later, in the next section.

387:                       4. Infinite tournaments
388: In this section we prove structure theorems for infinite tournaments.
389:   For any cardinal κ let the digraph Tκ,∞ = (κ, ≥), i.e. (x, y) is an
390: edge if and only if x ≥ y. (This is a tournament with all possible
391: loops.)

393: are equivalent:
394:   (i) G ∈/ Out∞ ,
395:  (ii) for some regular cardinal κ there is a surjective homomorphism
396:       ϕ : G → Tκ,∞ .

398: y ∈ OutG                   G
